Proof of Lemma
7

We follow the proof of Theorem 3.2 in
Belloni and
Oliveira 2018
, and make only one essential modification. Observe that
2^{2}>e
, so
\log 2>1/2
. This implies
(\log p)^{-1/2}<\sqrt{2}
for all
p\geq 2
.

First, consider the case that
\delta\geq s/(14\log(2)-1)
. Then
\delta>s/(14\log p)
and so

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j=1}^{p}-t\Big|\leq\delta\Big)\leq 1\leq 14\frac{\delta}{s}\log(p).

(91)

Now consider the case
\delta<s/(14\log(2)-1)
, and let
c=(s-\delta)/\sqrt{2}
. Divide the index set of the random variables
X_{j}
into two groups. Let
A_{1}=\{j|\sigma_{j}\geq c\log^{-1/2}(p)\}
, and let
A_{2}=\{j|\sigma_{j}<c\log^{-1/2}(p)\}
. By the union bound,

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j=1}^{p}-t\Big|\leq\delta\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j\in A_{1}}X_{j}-t\Big|\leq\delta\Big)+{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j\in A_{2}}X_{j}-t\Big|\leq\delta\Big).

(92)

We will bound each term on the right hand side separately, starting with
A_{2}
. Observe that the density function
f_{\sigma^{2}}(t)
of
N(0,\sigma^{2})
is non-decreasing in
\sigma
on
[0,t]
and is decreasing in
t
on
[0,\infty)
. Noting that
t-\delta\geq s-\delta\geq s/(14\log(2)-1)-\delta>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j\in A_{2}}X_{j}-t\Big|\leq\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in A_{2}}{\mathrm{P}}(|X_{j}-t|\leq\delta)

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta\sum_{j\in A_{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\sigma_{j}^{2}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{(t-\delta)^{2}}{2\sigma_{j}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta\sum_{j\in A_{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\sigma_{j}^{2}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{(s-\delta)^{2}}{2\sigma_{j}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=2\delta\sum_{j\in A_{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\sigma_{j}^{2}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{(\sqrt{2}c)^{2}}{2\sigma_{j}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta\sum_{j\in A_{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(c\log^{-1/2}(p))^{2}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{(\sqrt{2}c)^{2}}{2(c\log^{-1/2}(p))^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=2\delta\sum_{j\in A_{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}c}\log^{1/2}(p)\exp(-\log(p))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\delta\frac{1}{c}\log^{1/2}(p)=\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{\delta}{s-\delta}\log^{1/2}(p)\leq\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{\delta}{s-\delta}\log(p)\leq 2\frac{\delta}{s-\delta}\log(p).

since
|A_{2}|\leq p
. The fourth line follows from the third because
s-\delta\leq t-\delta
for all
t
. The fifth line follows from the fourth by solving for
s-\delta
in terms of
c
. The sixth line follows from the fifth due to our observation that
\sigma_{j}<\sqrt{2}c
, that therefore increasing
\sigma_{j}
to some value less than
\sqrt{2}c
causes the density to increase, and that finally by definition
c\log^{-1/2}(p)<\sqrt{2}c
. The second last inequality follows from
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log(p)}}\leq\sqrt{2}
. The constant could be improved by allowing only
p\geq 3
.

The term involving
A_{1}
is bounded as follows

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(|\max_{j\in A_{1}}X_{j}-t|\leq\delta\Big)\leq 2\delta\frac{\log^{1/2}(p)}{c}(\sqrt{2}\log^{1/2}(|A_{1}|)+2),

this is a direct consequence of Nazarov’s inequality (Lemma
6
) and the fact that
\min_{j\in A_{1}}\sigma_{j}\geq c\log^{-1/2}(p)
. Since
|A_{1}|\leq p
, we have:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(|\max_{j\in A_{1}}X_{j}-t|\leq\delta\Big)\leq 2\frac{\delta}{s-\delta}\sqrt{2}\log^{1/2}(p)(\sqrt{2}\log^{1/2}(p)+2)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{s-\delta}(4\log(p)+4\sqrt{2}\log(p)\log^{-1/2}(p))\leq\frac{\delta}{s-\delta}(4\log(p)+8\log(p))\leq 12\frac{\delta}{s-\delta}\log(p)

Therefore by Equation (
92
) we have shown that

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j=1}^{p}-t\Big|\leq\delta\Big)\leq 14\frac{\delta}{s-\delta}\log(p).

To complete the argument, note that
14\log(2)-1\geq 6
. Thus
\delta\leq s/(14\log(2)-1)\leq s/6
and
1/(s-\delta)\leq\tfrac{6}{5s}
. This implies

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j=1}^{p}-t\Big|\leq\delta\Big)\leq 14\frac{\delta}{s-\delta}\log(p)\leq\frac{84}{5}\frac{\delta}{s}\log(p).

This concludes the proof of Lemma
7
.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
9

First note that

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(S\in[t-\delta,t]\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\in E_{t}/E_{t-\delta}\Big).

Since
t-\delta>\varepsilon>0
, for any collection
\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta}\subset(\mathbb{R}^{M})^{K}
of Lemma
1
,

A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta},t-\delta-\varepsilon)\subset E_{t-\delta}\subset A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta},t-\delta).

Thus,

E_{t}=\frac{t}{t-\delta}E_{t-\delta}\subset\frac{t}{t-\delta}A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta},t-\delta)=A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta},t),

so that

A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta},t-\delta-\varepsilon)\subset E_{t-\delta}\subset E_{t}\subset A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta},t).

Hence

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(W\in E_{t})-P(W\in E_{t-\delta})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}(W\in A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta},t))-{\mathrm{P}}(W\in A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta},t-\delta-\varepsilon))

\displaystyle={\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta}}v^{\top}W\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta}}v^{\top}W\leq t-\delta-\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle={\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta}}v^{\top}W\in[t-\delta-\varepsilon,t]\Big).

By Lemma
7
and the bound
\log(|\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta}|)\leq 3M\log(K(1+(t-\delta)\varepsilon^{-1}))
(see Lemma
1
) the last line is bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{17}{2}\frac{\delta+\varepsilon}{t}\log(|\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t-\delta}|)\leq\frac{51}{2}\frac{\delta+\varepsilon}{t}M\log(K(1+(t-\delta)\varepsilon^{-1})).

This completes the proof of the first bound in the Lemma.

To prove the second statement of the Lemma, note that
Lemma
37
shows that for
C_{1}=\sqrt{8/3}K_{\psi_{1}}^{1/2}
( where
K_{\psi_{1}}
is the constant of Lemma
41
(ii)) if
t-\delta\geq C_{1}\sqrt{\log(K+1)}U\sqrt{\log(\frac{2}{\varepsilon})}
,

{\mathrm{P}}(W\in E_{t}/E_{t-\delta})={\mathrm{P}}(W\in[t-\delta,t])\leq{\mathrm{P}}(W\geq t-\delta)\leq\varepsilon.

If
t-\delta\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\log(K+1)}U\sqrt{\log(\frac{2}{\varepsilon})}
, use the general bound proved in the first part which gives

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(W\in E_{t}/E_{t-\delta})={\mathrm{P}}(W\in[t-\delta,t])\leq\frac{51}{2}\frac{\delta+\varepsilon}{t}M\log(K(1+(t-\delta)\varepsilon^{-1}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{51}{2}\frac{\delta+\varepsilon}{t}M\log\Big[K\Big(1+C_{1}U\varepsilon^{-1}\sqrt{\log(K+1)}\sqrt{\log(2/\varepsilon)}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{51}{2}\frac{\delta+\varepsilon}{t}M\log(K(1+(2C_{1}\sqrt{\log(K+1)}U\varepsilon^{-2}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{51}{2}\frac{\delta+\varepsilon}{t}M\log(K(1+(2C_{1}KU\varepsilon^{-2}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{51}{2}\frac{\delta+\varepsilon}{t}M\log\Big(\Big[\frac{(2(C_{1}\vee 1))(U\vee 1)K}{\varepsilon\wedge 1}\Big]^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=51\frac{\delta+\varepsilon}{t}M\log\Big(\frac{2(C_{1}\vee 1)(U\vee 1)K}{\varepsilon\wedge 1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 51\frac{\delta+\varepsilon}{t}M\log\Big(\frac{4(K_{\psi_{1}}^{1/2}\vee 1)(U\vee 1)K}{\varepsilon\wedge 1}\Big)

This completes the proof of Lemma
9
.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
10

We may re-order the components of
W_{k}
for every
1\leq k\leq K
without affecting the distribution of
S
; without loss of generality we assume that for all
1\leq k\leq K
and all
1\leq j\leq M
,
\sigma_{k,j+1}\leq\sigma_{k,j}
. We define
W_{k}:=\pi_{k}W
, so that
W_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
. We further assume without loss of generality that there is no pair
W_{k_{1}}
,
W_{k_{2}}
with
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
such that
W_{k_{1}}=OW_{k_{2}}
almost surely for an orthogonal transformation
O
, as in this case
\|W_{k_{2}}\|_{2}=\|W_{k_{1}}\|_{2}
almost surely.

Let
S^{M-1}=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{M}:\|v\|_{2}=1\}
. Then
S=\max_{k=1}^{K}\sup_{v\in S^{M-1}}v^{\top}W_{k}
. We will now define a family (indexed by
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
) of subsets of
S^{M-1}
called
V_{M}(\tau)
and a proxy for
S
called
\widetilde{S}
that will depend on
\tau
. Let

V_{M}(\tau)=\Big\{v\in S^{M-1}:\forall k,\sum_{j=1}^{M}v_{j}^{2}\sigma_{k,j}^{2}\geq\tau^{2}\Big\}

Let
\widetilde{S}(\tau)=\max_{k=1}^{K}\sup_{v\in V_{M}(\tau)}v^{\top}W_{k}
. The inequality
S\geq\widetilde{S}
holds (to lighten notation we do not always include the argument
\tau
). The left hand side of (
86
) can be broken into two pieces as follows:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\rho}{\mathrm{P}}(S\in[t,t+\delta])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\rho}{\mathrm{P}}(S\in[t,t+\delta],S\geq\rho)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\rho}\Big\{{\mathrm{P}}(S\in[t,t+\delta],S\geq\rho,S=\widetilde{S})+{\mathrm{P}}(S\in[t,t+\delta],S\geq\rho,S>\widetilde{S})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\rho}{\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}\in[t,t+\delta])+{\mathrm{P}}(S>\widetilde{S},S\geq\rho).

We will now bound

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\rho}{\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}\in[t,t+\delta])=\sup_{t\geq\rho}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\sup_{v\in V_{M}(\tau)}v^{\top}W_{k}\in[t,t+\delta]\Big)

in terms of the unspecified constant
\tau
. As a subset of the compact metric space
S^{M-1}
,
V_{M}(\tau)
is totally bounded. Let
\varepsilon_{\ell}\downarrow 0
, and let
\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{\ell}}\subset V_{M}(\tau)
be a finite
\varepsilon_{\ell}
-net for
V_{M}(\tau)
. Let
\widetilde{S}_{\ell}=\max_{k=1}^{K}\sup_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{\ell}}}v^{\top}W_{k}
. It is straightforward to see that
\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\uparrow\widetilde{S}
as
\ell\to\infty
:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{S}-\widetilde{S}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k=1}^{K}\Big\{\sup_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{\ell}}}\sup_{w:\|w-v\|\leq\varepsilon_{\ell}}w^{\top}W_{k}-\sup_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{\ell}}}v^{\top}W_{k}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k=1}^{K}\Big\{\sup_{v\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{\ell}}}\Big(\sup_{w:\|w-v\|\leq\varepsilon_{\ell}}w^{\top}W_{k}-v^{\top}W_{k}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{\ell}\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2},

and by choice of the sequence
\varepsilon_{\ell}
,
\varepsilon_{\ell}\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\downarrow 0
. Thus for each fixed
t
, and each fixed
\delta
, by the bounded convergence theorem applied to appropriate indicator functions,
\lim_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in[t,t+\delta])={\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}\in[t,t+\delta])
and so
{\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}\in[t,t+\delta])\leq\sup_{\ell}{\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in[t,t+\delta])
.

For any
v\in V_{M}(\tau)\subset S^{M-1}
and all
k
we have
\Var(v^{\top}W_{k})=\Var(\sum_{j=1}^{M}v_{j}[W_{k}]_{j})=\sum_{j=1}^{M}v_{j}^{2}\Var([W_{k}]_{j})\leq C_{1}^{2}
, and also
\Var(v^{\top}W_{k})\geq\tau^{2}
. By Lemma
8
, for a universal constant
D_{1}
and any
\ell
,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}{\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in[t,t+\delta])\leq D_{1}\frac{C_{1}}{\tau^{2}}\delta\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{k=1}^{K}\max_{v\in V_{M}(\tau)}\frac{v^{\top}W_{k}}{\sqrt{\Var(v^{\top}W_{k})}}\Big]+\sqrt{1\vee\log(\frac{\tau}{\delta})}\Big\}.

A straightforward computation using Lemma
42
(ii) and Lemma
41
(ii), gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{k=1}^{K}\max_{v\in V_{M}(\tau)}\frac{v^{\top}W_{k}}{\sqrt{\Var(v^{\top}W_{k})}}\Big]\leq\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{k=1}^{K}\max_{v\in V_{M}(\tau)}v^{\top}W_{k}\Big]\leq\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{k=1}^{K}\max_{v\in S^{M-1}}v^{\top}W_{k}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}\mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\Big]\leq C_{2,1}\frac{1}{\tau}\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C_{2,1}K^{1/2}_{\Psi_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(K+1)}}{\tau}\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\|W_{k}\|_{2}\|_{\Psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=C_{2,1}K^{1/2}_{\Psi_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(K+1)}}{\tau}\max_{k=1}^{K}(\|\|W_{k}\|^{2}_{2}\|_{\Psi_{1}})^{1/2}=C_{2,1}K^{1/2}_{\Psi_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(K+1)}}{\tau}\max_{k=1}^{K}(\|\sum_{j=1}^{M}[W_{k}]^{2}_{j}\|_{\Psi_{1}})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,1}K^{1/2}_{\Psi_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(K+1)}}{\tau}\max_{k=1}^{K}(\sum_{j=1}^{M}\|[W_{k}]^{2}_{j}\|_{\Psi_{1}})^{1/2}=C_{2,1}K^{1/2}_{\Psi_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(K+1)}}{\tau}\max_{k=1}^{K}(\sum_{j=1}^{M}\|[W_{k}]_{j}\|^{2}_{\Psi_{2}})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8/3}C_{2,1}K^{1/2}_{\Psi_{1}}C_{1}\frac{\sqrt{\log(K+1)}}{\tau}(\sum_{j=1}^{M}j^{-2\alpha})^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{8/3}\sqrt{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}}C_{2,1}K^{1/2}_{\Psi_{1}}C_{1}\frac{\sqrt{\log(K+1)}}{\tau}.

We used that if
X
is centered Gaussian with standard deviation
\sigma
that
\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}=\sqrt{8/3}\sigma
and the
L^{\psi_{1}}
triangle inequality, which follows from Lemma
38
, and bounded the finite sum in the last line with an infinite sum, and the constants
C_{2,1}
and
K_{\psi_{1}}
are defined in Lemmas
42
and
41
.
Defining
D_{2}=\sqrt{8/3}\sqrt{(2\alpha)/(2\alpha-1)}[C_{2,1}\vee 1][K_{\Psi_{1}}^{1/2}\vee 1]
we have

\mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{k=1}^{K}\max_{v\in V_{M}(\tau)}\frac{v^{\top}W_{k}}{\sqrt{\Var(v^{\top}W_{k})}}\Big]\leq C_{1}D_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log(K+1)}}{\tau}

(93)

and thus for any
\delta>0
,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}{\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}_{\ell}\in[t,t+\delta])\leq D_{1}C_{1}\frac{\delta}{\tau^{2}}\Big\{\frac{D_{2}C_{1}}{\tau}\sqrt{\log(K+1)}+\sqrt{1\vee\log(\frac{\tau}{\delta})}\Big\}.

Set

C_{\tau}^{-1}=\Big([K_{\psi_{1}}\vee 1](8/3)\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}C^{2}_{1}\Big)^{\beta/(2\alpha-1)}2^{(1+2\beta)/2+\beta/(2\alpha-1)}(1/C_{2})\rho^{-(2\beta)/(2\alpha-1)}

and let
\tau=C_{\tau}(\log(K+1)\log(2{m}))^{\frac{-\beta}{2\alpha-1}}
.
This choice of
C_{\tau}
is justified by Equation (
101
) below. Let

D_{3}=\Big((K_{\psi_{1}}\vee 1)(8/3)\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}\Big)^{\beta/(2\alpha-1)}2^{(1+2\beta)/2+\beta/(2\alpha-1)},

and let
\gamma=\beta/(2\alpha-1)
. Note for later that

C^{-1}_{\tau}=D_{3}(C_{1}/\rho)^{2\beta/(2\alpha-1)}(1/C_{2}).

(94)

With this choice of
\tau
we obtain,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}{\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}\in[t,t+\delta])\leq D_{1}D_{3}^{2}\frac{C_{1}^{4\gamma+1}}{C_{2}^{2}\rho^{4\gamma}}\delta[\log(K+1)]^{2\gamma}[\log(2{m})]^{2\gamma}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{D_{2}D_{3}\frac{C_{1}^{2\gamma+1}}{C_{2}\rho^{2\gamma}}[\log(K+1)]^{\gamma+\frac{1}{2}}[\log(2{m}))]^{\gamma}+\sqrt{1\vee\log(\tau/\delta)}\Big\}

Now note that the term
\sqrt{\log(\tau/\delta)}
is less than or equal to the first term in the same bracket if and only if

\displaystyle\delta\geq\frac{\frac{C_{2}\rho^{2\gamma}}{C_{1}^{2\gamma}D_{3}}}{[\log(K+1)]^{\gamma}[\log(2{m})]^{\gamma}\exp\Big(D_{2}^{2}D_{3}^{2}\frac{C_{1}^{4\gamma+2}}{C_{2}^{2}\rho^{4\gamma}}[\log(K+1)]^{2\gamma+1}[\log(2{m})]^{2\gamma}\Big)}

The left hand side of the preceding equation can be bounded as follows

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{C_{2}\rho^{2\gamma}}{C_{1}^{2\gamma}D_{3}}}{[\log(K+1)]^{\gamma}[\log(2{m})]^{\gamma}\exp\Big(D_{2}^{2}D_{3}^{2}\frac{C_{1}^{4\gamma+2}}{C_{2}^{2}\rho^{4\gamma}}[\log(K+1)]^{2\gamma+1}[\log(2{m})]^{2\gamma}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{\gamma}\frac{C_{2}\rho^{2\gamma}}{C_{1}^{2\gamma}D_{3}}}{[\log(2{m})]^{\gamma}\exp\Big((1/2)^{\gamma+1}D_{2}^{2}D_{3}^{2}\frac{C_{1}^{4\gamma+2}}{C_{2}^{2}\rho^{4\gamma}}[\log(2{m})]^{2\gamma}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}(\rho/C_{1})^{2\gamma}}{[\log(2{m})]^{\gamma}\exp\Big(\frac{C_{1}^{4\gamma+2}}{C_{2}^{2}\rho^{4\gamma}}[\log(2{m})]^{2\gamma}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{[\log({m})]^{\gamma}{m}}.

In the first inequality we used
\log(K+1)\geq\frac{1}{2}
both inside and outside of the argument of the exponential. In the second inequality we used
D_{2}\geq 1
and
\frac{D^{2}_{3}}{2^{\gamma+1}}\geq\frac{(8/3)^{2\gamma}}{2^{\gamma+1}}>\frac{2^{2\gamma}}{2^{\gamma+1}}\geq\frac{2^{\gamma+1}}{2^{\gamma+1}}=1
.
In the final inequality we used that
\rho\leq 1
and that
C_{1}\geq C_{2}
, that
C_{1}\geq 1
and everywhere use used
2\gamma>1
. It follows that for
\delta\geq\frac{1}{{m}[\log({m})]^{\gamma}}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}{\mathrm{P}}(\widetilde{S}\in[t,t+\delta])\leq 2D_{1}D_{2}D_{3}^{3}\frac{C_{1}^{6\gamma+2}}{C_{2}^{3}\rho^{6\gamma}}\delta[\log(K+1)]^{3\gamma+\frac{1}{2}}[\log(2{m})]^{3\gamma},

and plugging in the formula for
\gamma
in terms of
\alpha
and
\beta
given above yields the first summand in the right-hand side of Equation (
86
), after noting that the left-hand side of Equation (
86
) is non-decreasing in
\delta
.
Now we will show with this choice of
\tau
that

{\mathrm{P}}(S>\widetilde{S},S\geq\rho)\leq\frac{1}{{m}}.

(95)

With this result established, the proof will be complete upon setting the constant
D
in the formula of the Lemma we are proving equal to
4D_{1}D_{2}D_{3}^{3}/\log(2)^{3\gamma+\frac{1}{2}}
. We note that by Lemma
45
the set
\arg\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}
is almost surely a singleton. Let
\widehat{k}=\arg\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}
, and for
k=1,\dots,K
define
\widehat{v}_{k}=\frac{W_{k}}{\|W_{k}\|_{2}}
, and

s_{k}(v):=\sum_{j=1}^{M}v_{j}^{2}\sigma_{k,j}^{2}=\Var(v^{\top}W_{k}).

With this notation we have
S=\widehat{v}_{\widehat{k}}^{\top}W_{\widehat{k}}
, that for all
1\leq k\leq K
,
\widehat{v}_{k}=\arg\max_{v\in S^{M-1}}v^{\top}W_{k}
, and that

\{S>\widetilde{S}\}\subset\{\widehat{v}_{\widehat{k}}\notin V_{M}(\tau)\}=\{\exists k,s_{k}(\widehat{v}_{\widehat{k}})<\tau^{2}\},

(96)

which implies

\{S>\widetilde{S},S\geq\rho\}\subset\{\widehat{v}_{\widehat{k}}\notin V_{M}(\tau),S\geq\rho\}\subset\{\exists k,s_{k}(\widehat{v}_{\widehat{k}})<\tau^{2},S\geq\rho\}.

(97)

We will now construct an event
\Omega_{1}
with the property that
\Omega_{1}\subset\{\forall k,s_{k}(\widehat{v}_{\widehat{k}})\geq\tau^{2},S\geq\rho\}
, and also,
{\mathrm{P}}(\Omega_{1}^{c},S\geq\rho)\leq\frac{1}{{m}}.
Since

\Omega_{1}^{c}\cap\{S\geq\rho\}\supset(\{\exists k,s_{k}(\widehat{v}_{\widehat{k}})<\tau^{2}\}\cup\{S<\rho\})\cap\{S\geq\rho\}=\{\exists k,s_{k}(\widehat{v}_{\widehat{k}})\tau^{2},S\geq\rho\},

this implies (
95
). Let

L=\lfloor 2^{-1/(2\beta)}C_{2}^{1/\beta}\tau^{-1/\beta}\rfloor

The inequality
\frac{x}{2}\leq\lfloor x\rfloor\leq x
holds whenever
x\geq 2
, and so whenever

2^{-1/2\beta}C_{2}^{1/\beta}\tau^{-1/\beta}\geq 2,

(98)

we have

2^{-1/(2\beta)-1}C_{2}^{1/\beta}\tau^{-1/\beta}\leq L\leq 2^{-1/(2\beta)}C_{2}^{1/\beta}\tau^{-1/\beta}.

(99)

Note that
D_{3}\geq 2^{(1+2\beta)/2}2^{\beta/(2\alpha-1)}
and so

\begin{split}&2^{-1/2\beta}C_{2}^{1/\beta}\tau^{-1/\beta}=2^{-1/2\beta}C_{2}^{1/\beta}[D_{3}(C_{1}/\rho)^{2\beta/(2\alpha-1)}(1/C_{2})]^{1/\beta}[\log(K+1)\log(2{m})]^{\frac{1}{2\alpha-1}}\\
&\geq 2^{-1/2\beta}[2^{(1+2\beta)/2}2^{\beta/(2\alpha-1)}]^{1/\beta}[\log(K+1)\log(2{m})]^{\frac{1}{2\alpha-1}}\\
&\geq 2[\log(2{m})]^{\frac{1}{2\alpha-1}},\end{split}

(100)

where in the last line we used
\log(K+1)\geq 1/2
. Therefore it suffices that
\log(2{m})\geq 1
and so it suffices that
{m}\geq 2
for (
99
) to hold.
Let

\Omega_{1}:=\Big\{\sum_{j=L+1}^{M}[W_{\widehat{k}}]_{j}^{2}\leq\rho^{2}/2,S^{2}\geq\rho^{2}\Big\}.

Since
S^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{M}[W_{\widehat{k}}]_{j}^{2}
, on the event
\Omega_{1}
,
\sum_{j=1}^{L}[W_{\widehat{k}}]_{j}^{2}\geq S^{2}/2.
Thus on the event
\Omega_{1}
, for all
1\leq k\leq K
,

s_{k}(\widehat{v}_{\widehat{k}})=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sigma_{k,j}^{2}[W_{\widehat{k}}]^{2}_{j}}{S^{2}}\geq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{L}\sigma_{k,j}^{2}[W_{\widehat{k}}]^{2}_{j}}{2\sum_{j=1}^{L}[W_{\widehat{k}}]_{j}^{2}}\geq\frac{\sigma_{k,L}^{2}}{2}\geq C_{2}^{2}L^{-2\beta}/2\geq\tau^{2}.

For all
1\leq j\leq M
the variable
[W_{k}]_{j}
is Gaussian. A straightforward computation using Lemma
42
and Lemma
41
, similar to the derivation of (
93
) earlier in this proof, noting that
2\alpha\geq 1
, gives

\displaystyle\Big\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=L+1}^{M}[W_{k}]_{j}^{2}\Big\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq K_{\psi_{1}}(8/3)\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}C^{2}_{1}\log(K+1)L^{-2\alpha+1}

\displaystyle\leq[K_{\psi_{1}}\vee 1](8/3)\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}C^{2}_{1}\log(K+1)2^{(2\alpha-1)(2\beta+1)/(2\beta)}C^{-(2\alpha-1)/(\beta)}_{2}\tau^{(2\alpha-1)/\beta},

where in the last line we plugged in the lower bound on
L
from (
99
).
Recall that
\tau=C_{\tau}(\log(K+1)\log(2{m}))^{\frac{-\beta}{2\alpha-1}}
, where the exact form of the constant
C_{\tau}
is defined above. Plugging this into the last line gives

\Big\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=L+1}^{M}[W_{k}]_{j}^{2}\Big\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\rho^{2}[2\log(2{m})]^{-1},

(101)

and so, using Lemma
41
(iv)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(\Omega_{1}^{c},S\geq\rho)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\sum_{j=L+1}^{M}[W_{\widehat{k}}]_{j}^{2}\geq\rho^{2}/2\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=L+1}^{M}[W_{k}]_{j}^{2}\geq\rho^{2}/2\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp(-\log(2{m}))=\frac{1}{{m}}.

\Box

Proof of Corollary
1

If
\delta\geq t/2
, we have
{\mathrm{P}}\Big(S^{2}\in[t-\delta,t]\Big)\leq 2\delta/t
, from which the bound readily follows. Therefore we may assume that
\delta<t/2
. Then,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(S^{2}\in[t-\delta,t]\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(S\in[\sqrt{t-\delta},\sqrt{t}]\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(S\in[\sqrt{t}-\frac{\delta}{2\sqrt{t-\delta}},\sqrt{t}]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 51\Big\{\frac{\delta/(2\sqrt{t-\delta})+\varepsilon}{\sqrt{t}}M\log\Big(\frac{4(K_{\Psi_{1}}\vee 1)^{\frac{1}{2}}K(U\vee 1)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big\}\vee\varepsilon

\displaystyle=51\Big\{\frac{\delta/2+\varepsilon\sqrt{t-\delta}}{\sqrt{t}\sqrt{t-\delta}}M\log\Big(\frac{4(K_{\Psi_{1}}\vee 1)^{\frac{1}{2}}K(U\vee 1)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big\}\vee\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq 51\Big\{\frac{\delta/2+\varepsilon\sqrt{t-\delta}}{t-\delta}M\log\Big(\frac{4(K_{\Psi_{1}}\vee 1)^{\frac{1}{2}}K(U\vee 1)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big\}\vee\varepsilon.

In the first line we used the mean-value theorem, and in the second line we used Lemma
9
. Note that to use Lemma
9
in this setting we require

\displaystyle\delta/(2\sqrt{t-\delta})+\varepsilon\leq\sqrt{t},

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\delta+2\sqrt{t-\delta}\varepsilon\leq 2\sqrt{t-\delta}\sqrt{t},

which is implied by

\displaystyle\delta+2\sqrt{t-\delta}\varepsilon\leq 2(t-\delta),

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\varepsilon\leq(2t-3\delta)/2\sqrt{t-\delta},

which is what we assumed in the statement of the Corollary.
\Box

Proof of Corollary
2

We will treat the cases
\delta\geq\rho/2
and
\delta<\rho/2
separately. When
\delta\geq\rho/2
,

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}^{\frac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}+2}}{C_{2}^{3}(\rho)^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}+\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(\delta\vee\frac{1}{{m}[\log({m})]^{\beta/2\alpha-1}}\Big)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}({m})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta}{\rho}\frac{1}{\rho^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}-\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{C_{1}^{3}}{C_{2}^{3}}C_{1}^{\frac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}-1}2^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2\beta}{2\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle\geq 2\cdot 2^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2\beta}{2\alpha-1}}

where in the last line we used
{m}\geq 3,\rho<1,C_{1}/C_{2}\geq 1,C_{1}\geq 1,\tfrac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}>1
. Thus the conclusion of the proposition holds in this case by picking
D\geq 2^{\frac{2\beta}{2\alpha-1}-\frac{1}{2}}
.

In the case
\delta<\rho/2
, note that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(S^{2}\in[t-\delta,t+\delta])={\mathrm{P}}\big(S\in[\sqrt{t-\delta},\sqrt{t+\delta}]\big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(S^{2}\in\Big[\sqrt{t-\delta},\sqrt{t-\delta}+\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t-\delta}}\Big]\Big).

We used the bound
|\sqrt{t+\delta}-\sqrt{t-\delta}|\leq\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t-\delta}}
which follows from the mean value theorem.
Since
\delta<\rho/2
and
t\geq\rho
,
\sqrt{t-\delta}\geq\sqrt{\rho/2}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\rho}{\mathrm{P}}(S^{2}\in[t-\delta,t+\delta])\leq\sup_{t\geq\sqrt{\rho/2}}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(S\in\Big[t,t+\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t-\delta}}\Big]\Big)

Then by Lemma
10
, denoting the constant
D
of Lemma
10
by
D_{2}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\rho}{\mathrm{P}}(S\in[t-\delta,t+\delta])

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D_{2}C_{1}^{\frac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}+2}}{C_{2}^{3}(\sqrt{\rho/2})^{\frac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}\Big(\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{\rho/2}}\vee\frac{1}{{m}[\log({m})]^{\frac{\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}\Big)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}({m})+\frac{1}{{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}+\frac{1}{2}}D_{2}C_{1}^{\frac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}+2}}{C_{2}^{3}(\rho\wedge 1)^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}+\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(\delta\vee\frac{1}{{m}[\log({m})]^{\frac{\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}\Big)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}({m})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}+\frac{1}{2}}D_{2}C_{1}^{\frac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}+2}}{C_{2}^{3}(\rho)^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}+\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(\delta\vee\frac{1}{{m}[\log({m})]^{\frac{\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}\Big)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}({m})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{DC_{1}^{\frac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}+2}}{C_{2}^{3}(\rho)^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}+\frac{1}{2}}}\Big(\delta\vee\frac{1}{{m}[\log({m})]^{\frac{\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}\Big)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}({m})

where
D
is the larger of
2^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}+\frac{1}{2}}D_{2}
and
2^{\frac{2\beta}{2\alpha-1}-\frac{1}{2}}
. This completes the proof.

\Box

Proof of Lemma
11

Set
D^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}=F^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}=\frac{4}{\log^{1+{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}(2)}\frac{1}{q}
, and note that if
\delta\geq q/4
, it follows that

\sup_{t\geq q}{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}W\in[t-\delta,t+\delta]\Big)\leq 1\leq D^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}\delta\log^{\frac{1}{2}+{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}({m}),

(102)

and also that

\sup_{t\geq q}\sup_{w\geq q/2}{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}W\in[w-\delta,w+\delta]\Big)\leq 1\leq F^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}\delta\log^{1+{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}({m}).

(103)

We now consider the case
\delta<q/4
. Let
\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}
denote a collection from Lemma
1
. For each
t>0
,
\delta>0
and
w>0
,
\varepsilon>0
satisfying
t\wedge\delta>\varepsilon>0
,

A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t},w+\delta)=\frac{w+\delta}{t}A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t},t)\subset\frac{w+\delta}{t}E_{t+\varepsilon}=E_{w+\delta+w\varepsilon t^{-1}+\varepsilon\delta t^{-1}}\subset E_{w+\delta(2+wt^{-1})},

where we used
\varepsilon\leq\delta
.

Since
\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}
is a collection of unit vectors it holds by definition that
E_{w-\delta}\subset A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t},w-\delta)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}W\in[w-\delta,w+\delta]\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}W\leq w+\delta\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}W<w-\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}(W\in E_{w+\delta(2+wt^{-1})})-{\mathrm{P}}(W\in E_{w-\delta})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\in[w-\delta,w+\delta(2+wt^{-1})]\Big).

If
w=t
, by Lemma
10
, for any
t\geq q
, there is a constant
A_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1},C_{2}}
such that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\in[w-\delta,w+\delta(2+wt^{-1})]\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\in[t-\delta,t+3\delta]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\geq q/2}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\in[t,t+4\delta]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq A_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1},C_{2}}q^{-\frac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}}\Big((4\delta)\vee(1/{m})\Big)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}({m}).

Now set
D_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}^{(2)}=4A_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1},C_{2}}q^{-\frac{6\beta}{2\alpha-1}}
, and set
D_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}
in the formulation of the Lemma equal to
D_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}:=D^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}\vee D^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}
. This proves the first claim by demonstrating the bound in Equation (
88
).

We now consider the case
w\neq t
, on the way to justifying the second claim. By Lemma
37
there is a constant
C_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1}}
such that if
w-\delta\geq C_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1}}\sqrt{\log(K+1)}\sqrt{\log(2{m})}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\in[w-\delta,w+\delta(2+wt^{-1})]\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\geq w-\delta\Big)\leq\frac{1}{{m}}.

Thus for any
t>\varepsilon>0
and any
\delta>0
and
w\geq\delta+C_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1}}\sqrt{\log{K+1}}\sqrt{\log(2{m})}
we have

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}W\in[w-\delta,w+\delta]\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\geq w-\delta\Big)\leq\delta\vee(1/{m}).

(104)

Set
F^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}=\frac{1}{\log^{1+{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}(2)}
so that

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}W\in[t-\delta,t+\delta]\Big)\leq F^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}(\delta\vee(1/{m}))\log^{1+{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}({m}).

(105)

Now suppose
q/2\leq w<\delta+C_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1}}\sqrt{\log(K+1)}\sqrt{\log(2{m})}
and
t\geq q
. Recall that we are in the case
\delta\leq q/4<1
. Then,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}W\in[w-\delta,w+\delta]\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\in[w-\delta,w+\delta(2+wt^{-1})]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\geq q/4}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\in[x,x+\delta(3+wt^{-1})]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\geq q/4}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\in\Big[x,x+\frac{\delta}{q}\Big\{4+C_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1}}\sqrt{\log(K+1)}\sqrt{\log(2{m})}\Big\}\Big]\Big).

We have used
q\leq 1
,
q\leq t
and
\delta<1
in the last line. Set
C^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1}}=4\sqrt{2}+C_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1}}
so that we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}W\in[w-\delta,w+\delta]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\geq q/4}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|W_{k}\|_{2}\in\Big[x,x+\frac{C^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta,C_{1}}\delta}{q}\sqrt{\log{(K+1)}}\sqrt{\log(2{m})}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq F^{(3)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}(\delta\vee(1/{m}))\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}+1}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}+\frac{1}{2}}({m}),

where
F^{(3)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}
is a constant that follows from combining the previous constant with the constant from Lemma
10
. Then set
F_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}
in the formulation of the Lemma as
F_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}=F^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}\vee F^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}\vee F^{(3)}_{\alpha,\beta,q,C_{1},C_{2}}
. This completes the proof of Lemma
11
.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
12

By Lemma
1
,

A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t},t-\varepsilon)\subset E_{t}\subset A(\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t},t).

Thus

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(X\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}(Y\in E_{t})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}X\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}Y\leq t-\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}X\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}Y\leq t\Big)+\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}Y\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}Y\leq t-\varepsilon\Big)\Big|,

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(Y\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}(X\in E_{t})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}Y\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}X\leq t-\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}Y\leq t-\varepsilon\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}X\leq t-\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}Y\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}Y\leq t-\varepsilon\Big)\Big|.

Hence,

\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(Y\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}(X\in E_{t})\Big|\leq I_{1}(t,\varepsilon)+I_{2}(t,\varepsilon).

\Box

S.6.5
Gaussian approximation results revisited

Let
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be an independent collection of centered variables taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Let
\{Y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be an independent collection of centered Gaussian variables with
\Var(Y_{i})=\Var(X_{i})
. Let
1\leq B_{n}<\infty
satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{j=1}^{p}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|[X_{i}]_{j}|^{3}]\leq B_{n},

(106)

\displaystyle\max_{j=1}^{p}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|[X_{i}]_{j}|^{4}]\leq B_{n}^{2},

(107)

\displaystyle\max_{i=1}^{n}\max_{j=1}^{p}\|[X_{i}]_{j}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq B_{n}.

(108)

Define
S^{Y}_{n}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}
and
S^{X}_{n}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}
. The following is modified version of Proposition 2.1 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
:

Theorem 3
(Gaussian Approximation on Rectangles Revisited – Abstract Version).

Let

\rho_{n}(t):=\sup_{x\geq t}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}[S^{X}_{n}]_{j}\leq x\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}[S^{Y}_{n}]_{j}\leq x\Big)\Big|.

For
s\in\mathbb{R}
, define

\mathcal{A}(s,\delta):=\sup_{x\geq s}{\mathrm{P}}(|\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{Y}]_{j}-x|\leq\delta).

Let
\phi_{*}=\Big(\frac{\log^{4}(p)B_{n}^{2}}{n}\Big)^{1/6}
. There is a universal constant
C
such that for all
p\geq 3
and all
n\geq 3
,

\rho_{n}(t)\leq C\max\Big\{\phi_{*}\log^{1/2}(n),B_{n}\mathcal{A}\big(t-C\log_{2}(n)\phi_{*},C\phi_{*}\big)\Big\}.

(109)

Note that the anti-concentration function
\mathcal{A}
is given a different definition in Equation (
40
), and we use the other definition in the proof of Theorem
1
and Theorem
2
.

Proof of Theorem
3

The first part of the proof closely follows the steps in the proof of Proposition 2.1 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
and we will only point out the places where modifications need to be made in order to obtain our version of the result. We begin by introducing additional notation which is as close as possible to that in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
in order to make it easier to compare our and their results. Let

L_{n}:=\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathrm{E}}[|X_{ij}|^{3}].

For all
\phi\geq 1
, define

M_{n,X}(\phi):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathrm{E}}\left[\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|X_{ij}|^{3}1\left\{\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|X_{ij}|>\sqrt{n}/(4\phi\log p)\right\}\right].

(110)

Similarly, define
M_{n,Y}(\phi)
with
X_{ij}
replaced by
Y_{ij}
in (
110
), and let

M_{n}(\phi):=M_{n,X}(\phi)+M_{n,Y}(\phi).

Following the analysis of
M_{n}(\phi)
in the proof of Proposition 2.1 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
in their case (E.1), we see that under (
106
)–(
108
) the following bound holds for universal constants
C_{2},C_{3}

M_{n}(\phi)\leq C_{3}\Big(\sqrt{n}/(\phi\log p)+B_{n}\log p\Big)^{3}\exp\Big(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{4C_{2}\phi B_{n}\log^{2}p}\Big).

(111)

Define

\varrho_{n}(c):=\sup_{x\geq c,v\in[0,1]}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\sqrt{v}S^{X}_{n}+\sqrt{1-v}S^{Y}_{n}\leq x\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}S^{Y}_{n}\leq x)\Big|,

where the random vectors
Y_{1},\dots,Y_{n}
are assumed to be independent of the random vectors
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
. We first state an intermediate bound on
\varrho_{n}(c)
which can be seen as a modification of Lemma 5.1 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{1}
such that for all
\phi\geq 1

\varrho_{n}(c)\leq C_{1}\frac{\phi^{3}(\log p)^{2}}{n^{1/2}}\Big\{M_{n}(\phi)+{L_{n}\mathcal{A}}(c-2/\phi,5/\phi)+L_{n}\varrho_{n}(c-8\phi^{-1})\Big\}+\mathcal{A}(c-2/\phi,1/\phi).

(112)

The derivation of this result relies on arguments from the proof of Lemma 5.1 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
with several modifications which will be discussed in detail below. Before providing this proof we complete the proof of our main result taking (
112
) as given.

Note that equation (
112
) can be restated as

\varrho_{n}(c)\leq a_{n}(\phi)\varrho_{n}(c-8/\phi)+b_{n}(\phi,c)

where
a_{n}(\phi)=C_{1}\frac{B_{n}\phi^{3}(\log p)^{2}}{n^{1/2}}
,

b_{n}(\phi,c)=C_{1}\frac{\phi^{3}(\log p)^{2}}{n^{1/2}}\Big\{M_{n}(\phi)+{L_{n}\mathcal{A}}(c-2/\phi,5/\phi)\Big\}+\mathcal{A}(c-2/\phi,1/\phi).

Note that above we have used the bound
L_{n}\leq B_{n}
, which holds by definition. Iteratively plugging applying this equation
M
times and noting that
b_{n}(\phi,c)
is non-increasing in
c
for every
\phi
(this follows by definition of
\mathcal{A}(t,\delta)
as a supremum over
[t,\infty)
) yields

\displaystyle\varrho_{n}(c)

\displaystyle\leq a_{n}(\phi)\varrho_{n}(c-8/\phi)+b_{n}(\phi,c)

\displaystyle\leq a_{n}(\phi)\{a_{n}(\phi)\varrho_{n}(c-16/\phi)+b_{n}(\phi,c-8/\phi)\}+b_{n}(\phi,c)

\displaystyle\leq a_{n}(\phi)^{M}\varrho_{n}(c-8M/\phi)+\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}a_{n}(\phi)^{j}b_{n}(\phi,c-8j/\phi)

\displaystyle\leq a_{n}(\phi)^{M}+b_{n}(\phi,c-8M/\phi)\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}a_{n}(\phi)^{j},

where we used the bound
\varrho_{n}(c)\leq 1
for every
c
in the last line. Now pick

\phi=\phi^{*}:=\Big(\frac{n}{4C_{1}^{2}\log^{4}(p)B_{n}^{2}}\Big)^{1/6}

resulting in
a_{n}(\phi)=1/2
and let
M=\lceil\log_{2}(n)\rceil
where
\log_{2}
denotes the logarithm with base
2
. This yields the bound

\varrho_{n}(c)\leq n^{-1}+2b_{n}(\phi^{*},c-8\lceil\log_{2}(n)\rceil/\phi^{*}).

We may assume without loss of generality that
\log^{1/2}(n)/\phi^{*}\leq C_{b}
for any universal constant
C_{b}
by (
109
), and we choose the value of
C_{b}
below. This implies that
\big[\frac{4C^{2}_{1}}{C_{b}^{6}}\big]B_{n}^{2}\log^{2}(p)\leq n
, and so
B_{n}\log(p)\leq(C^{6}_{b}n/4C^{2}_{1})^{1/2}
. By (
111
),

\displaystyle M_{n}(\phi^{*})\leq C_{3}\Big(\sqrt{n}/(\phi^{*}\log p)+B_{n}\log p\Big)^{3}\exp\Big(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{4C_{2}\phi^{*}B_{n}\log^{2}p}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\Big(\sqrt{n}/(C_{b}\log^{1/2}(n)\log p))+(C_{b}^{6}n/4C^{2}_{1})^{1/2}\Big)^{3}\exp\Big(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{4C_{2}\phi^{*}B_{n}\log^{2}p}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}n^{3/2}\exp\Big(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{4C_{2}\phi^{*}B_{n}\log^{2}p}\Big)

for a universal constant
C_{4}
. Then,

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{4C_{2}\phi^{*}B_{n}\log^{2}p}\Big)=\exp\Big(-(4C_{1}^{2})^{1/6}\frac{n^{1/3}}{4C_{2}B_{n}^{2/3}\log^{4/3}p}\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(-(\phi^{*})^{2}\frac{{(4C_{1}^{2})^{1/2}}}{4C_{2}}\Big)\leq\exp(-2\log(n))\leq n^{-2},

where we set
C_{b}
such that it implies that
(\phi^{*})^{2}\frac{{(4C_{1}^{2})^{1/2}}}{4C_{2}}\geq 2\log(n)
. It follows that
M_{n}(\phi^{*})\leq C_{4}n^{-1/2}
. Continuing we have

\displaystyle\varrho_{n}(t)\leq n^{-1}+2b_{n}(\phi^{*},t-8\lceil\log_{2}(n)\rceil/\phi^{*})

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}+2\big\{M_{n}(\phi^{*})+B_{n}\mathcal{A}(t-18\log_{2}(n)/\phi_{*},5/\phi^{*})\big\}+\mathcal{A}(t-18\log_{2}(n)/\phi_{*},5/\phi^{*})

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}+2C_{4}n^{-1/2}+2B_{n}\mathcal{A}(t-18\log_{2}(n)/\phi_{*},5/\phi^{*}),

where we used that
B_{n}
is and upper bound on
L_{n}
and
B_{n}>1
. By picking
C
to be a sufficiently large universal constant we obtain (
109
).

It remains to prove (
112
). To this end introduce the following definitions from
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
:
\beta:=\phi\log p
,

F_{\beta}(w,x):=\beta^{-1}\log\left({\textstyle\sum}_{j=1}^{p}\exp\left(\beta(w_{j}-x)\right)\right),\ w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}.

(113)

Note that in (
131
) we give the function
F_{\beta}
, a different definition, and the definition of (
113
) is used only within the body of the current proof.
The function
F_{\beta}(w,x)
can be considered as a smoothed version of
w\mapsto\max_{1\leq j\leq p}(w_{j}-x)
and satisfies (see equation (22) in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
)

0\leq F_{\beta}(w,x)-\max_{1\leq j\leq p}(w_{j}-x)\leq\beta^{-1}\log p=\phi^{-1},\ \text{for all}\ w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}.

(114)

Pick a three times continuously differentiable function
g_{0}:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
whose derivatives up to the third order are all bounded such that
g_{0}(t)=1
for
t\leq 0
and
g_{0}(t)=0
for
t\geq 1
, i.e. a smoothed version of the indicator function
I\{\cdot\leq 0\}
. Define
g(t):=g_{0}(\phi t),t\in\mathbb{R}
and set

m(w,x):=g(F_{\beta}(w,x)),\ w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}.

Further, let

\displaystyle h_{x}(w,t):=1\left\{-\phi^{-1}-t/\beta<\max_{1\leq j\leq p}(w_{j}-x)\leq\phi^{-1}+t/\beta\right\},\ w\in\mathbb{R}^{p},t>0,

(115)

\displaystyle\omega(t):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}\wedge\sqrt{1-t}},\ t\in(0,1).

Let

\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x):=m(\sqrt{v}S^{X}_{n}+\sqrt{1-v}S^{Y}_{n},x)-m(S^{W}_{n},x).

where
S^{W}_{n}
is based on independent copies
W_{i}
of
Y_{i}
define

V_{n}(v):=\sqrt{v}S^{X}_{n}+\sqrt{1-v}S^{Y}_{n}.

Then we have for all
v\in[0,1]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[V_{n}(v)]_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathrm{P}}(F_{\beta}(V_{n}(v),x)\leq 0)

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathrm{E}}[m(V_{n}(v),x)]

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathrm{P}}(F_{\beta}(S_{n}^{W},x)\leq\phi^{-1})+(\mathbb{E}[m(V_{n}(v),x)]-\mathbb{E}[m(S_{n}^{W},x)])

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}([S_{n}^{W}]_{j}-x)\leq\phi^{-1})+|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x)]|

\displaystyle\quad={\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}\leq\phi^{-1}+x)+|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x)]|

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1})+{\mathrm{P}}(|\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}-x|\leq\phi^{-1})+|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x)]|.

where the inequality symbol is applied element wise in the final line. We used (
114
) for the first inequality, the definition of
m
in the second and third inequality. The fourth inequality follows from (
114
) and the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{n}
. We note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[m(V_{n}(v),x-2\phi^{-1})]\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(F_{\beta}(V_{n}(v),x-2\phi^{-1})\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{j=1}^{p}V_{n}(v)_{j}-(x-2\phi^{-1})\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{j=1}^{p}[V_{n}(v)]_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1}\Big)

The inequality on the first line follows from the identity

m(V_{n}(v),x-2\phi^{-1})=g\circ F_{\beta}(V_{n}(v),x-2\phi^{-1})\leq\mathbf{1}\{F_{\beta}(V_{n}(v),x-2\phi^{-1})\leq\phi^{-1}\},

after taking expectations. The equality on the second line follow from the inequality

F_{\beta}(V_{n}(v),x-2\phi^{-1})\geq\max_{j=1}^{p}[V_{n}(v)]_{j}-(x-2\phi^{-1}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1})-{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[V_{n}(v)]_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1})-(\mathbb{E}[m(V_{n}(v),x-2\phi^{-1})]-\mathbb{E}[m(S_{n}^{W},x-2\phi^{-1})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\mathbb{E}[m(S_{n}^{W},x-2\phi^{-1})]

\displaystyle\quad\leq|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x-2\phi^{-1})]|+{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1})-{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}\leq x-3\phi^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad\leq|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x-2\phi^{-1})]|+{\mathrm{P}}(|\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}-x-2\phi^{-1}|\leq\phi^{-1})

We note that
{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}\leq x-3\phi^{-1})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(F_{\beta}(S_{n}^{W},x-2\phi^{-1})\leq 0\Big)\leq\mathbb{E}[m(S_{n}^{W},x-2\phi^{-1})]
, justifying the inequality between the third last and second last line.
Combining the inequalities above we find that for every
x\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[V_{n}(v)]_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1})-{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}(|\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}-x-2\phi|\leq\phi^{-1})\vee{\mathrm{P}}(|\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{W}]_{j}-x|\leq\phi^{-1})

(116)

\displaystyle\quad+|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x)]|\vee|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x-2\phi^{-1})]|.

It remains to bound
|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x)]|
. To this end, a close look at Step 1 in the proof of Lemma 5.1 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
reveals that the arguments used to bound
I,II,III_{2}
in the notation from that paper remain unchanged in our setting and thus for a universal constant
C_{4}

|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x)]|\leq C_{4}M_{n}(\phi)\phi\beta^{2}/n^{1/2}+|III_{1}|.

The bound for
III_{1}
takes the form

III_{1}\leq C_{5}M_{n}(\phi)\phi\beta^{2}/n^{1/2}+C_{5}\phi\beta^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}[h(Z(t,v),2)]\frac{\omega(t)L_{n}}{n^{1/2}}dt

where
C_{5}
denotes a universal constant and

\displaystyle Z(t,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\sqrt{tv}X_{i}+\sqrt{t(1-v)}Y_{i}+\sqrt{1-t}W_{i})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\sqrt{tv}X_{i}+\sqrt{1-tv}Y_{i}),

with
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}
denoting equality in distribution. Now for any
x>\phi^{-1}+2/\beta
, by definition of
h_{x}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[h_{x}(Z(t,v),2)]

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{i=1,\dots,p}Z_{i}(t,v)-x\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}+2/\beta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{i=1,\dots,p}Z_{i}(0,0)-x\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}+2/\beta\Big)

\displaystyle+2\sup_{y\geq x-\phi^{-1}-3/\beta}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{i=1,\dots,p}Z_{i}(0,0)\leq y\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{i=1,\dots,p}Z_{i}(t,v)\leq y\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(x,\phi^{-1}+2/\beta)+2\varrho_{n}(x-\phi^{-1}-3/\beta),

where we used the fact that
Z(0,0)=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}
. Combining the bounds above we obtain the inequality

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x)]|\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle(C_{4}+C_{5})M_{n}(\phi)\phi\beta^{2}/n^{1/2}

\displaystyle+C_{6}\phi\beta^{2}L_{n}n^{-1/2}\Big\{\mathcal{A}(x,\phi^{-1}+2/\beta)+\varrho_{n}(x-\phi^{-1}-3/\beta)\Big\}

where
C_{6}=2\times C_{5}\int_{0}^{1}\omega(t)dt
. We note that
\varrho_{n}(c)
is a decreasing function of
c
and so this bound is decreasing in
x
. We may therefore write

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x)]|\vee|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x-2\phi^{-1})]|

\displaystyle\leq(C_{4}+C_{5})M_{n}(\phi)\phi\beta^{2}/n^{1/2}

\displaystyle+2C_{6}\phi\beta^{2}L_{n}n^{-1/2}\Big\{(\mathcal{A}(x-2/\phi,\phi^{-1}+2/\beta)+\varrho_{n}(x-3\phi^{-1}-3/\beta))\Big\}

Finally, note that
2/\log p\leq 4,3/\log p\leq 5
and recall
\beta=\phi\log p
so that

|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x)]|\vee|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}_{n}(v,x-2\phi^{-1})]|\leq C_{7}\frac{\phi^{3}(\log p)^{2}}{n^{1/2}}\Big\{M_{n}(\phi)+L_{n}\mathcal{A}(x-2/\phi,5/\phi)+L_{n}\varrho_{n}(c-8\phi^{-1})\Big\}

for a universal constant
C_{7}
. Combining this with (
S.6.5
) yields

\varrho_{n}(c)\leq C_{8}\frac{\phi^{3}(\log p)^{2}}{n^{1/2}}\Big\{M_{n}(\phi)+L_{n}\mathcal{A}(c-2/\phi,5/\phi)+L_{n}\varrho_{n}(c-8\phi^{-1})\Big\}+\mathcal{A}(c-2/\phi,1/\phi).

This proves (
112
) and completes the proof of Theorem
3
.

\Box

S.6.6
Gaussian Approximation for squared
L^{\infty}-L^{2}
Mixed Norms

We begin this section by stating Lemmas that are used in the proof of Theorem
1
. Recall the definitions of
T^{M}
and
T^{MG}
made in Section
S.4
.

Lemma 13
.

Suppose conditions (A1) – (A3) hold. There exists a constant
C=C(C_{\phi},\alpha)
such that for all
s>0
, all
n\geq 3
, all
K\geq 1
and all
M\geq 3
,

\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(T^{MG}\leq t)-{\mathrm{P}}(T^{M}\leq t)\Big|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{s\wedge 1}}\Big(\dfrac{M^{10}\log^{10}(Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}.

(117)

Lemma 14
.

Suppose conditions (A1)–(A4) hold. There exists a constant
C=C(C_{\phi},C_{2},\alpha,\beta,s)
such that for all
n\geq 3
, and all
K\geq 1
and all
M\geq 3
,

\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(T^{MG}\leq t)-{\mathrm{P}}(T^{M}\leq t)\Big|\leq C\Big(\dfrac{M^{4}\log^{4}(Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}\log^{1+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(n).

(118)

Let
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be an independent collection of centered variables taking values in
(\mathbb{R}^{M})^{K}
. Let
\{Y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be an independent collection of centered Gaussian variables with
\Var(Y_{i})=\Var(X_{i})
. Let
1\leq H_{n}<\infty
satisfy

\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}\max_{i=1}^{n}\|[\pi_{k}X_{i}]_{j}\|^{2}_{\Psi_{1}}}\leq H_{n}.

(119)

Theorem 4
(Gaussian Approximation for Maxima of
L^{2}
norms – Abstract Version).

Let

\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(s,\delta):=\sup_{x\geq s}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\frac{1}{n},t}}v^{\top}S_{n}^{Y}-x\Big|\leq\delta\Big).

There exist universal constants
D
and
D_{1}
such that if
M\geq 3
, and
K\geq 1
,
n>2\vee(1/s)
,

\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(S^{Y}_{n}\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}(S^{X}_{n}\in E_{t})\Big|\leq D\Big[\Big\{\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}H_{n}^{3}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(s-D\log(n)\delta_{*},D\delta_{*})\Big\}\vee\big\{\delta_{*}\sqrt{\log(n)}\big\}\Big],

for
t_{*}=D_{1}H_{n}\log(K+1)\log(n)
and
\delta_{*}=\Big(\dfrac{H_{n}^{6}M^{4}\log^{4}(H_{n}Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}
.

Lemmas
13
and
14
are specializations of Theorem
4
using bounds on
\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(w,\delta)
that are available under (A1)–(A3) and (A1) –(A4) respectively.

Proof of Theorem
4

We specify the value of
D_{1}
in the formulation of the Theorem we are proving at the end of the proof. Let

\displaystyle I_{1}:=\sup_{t>t_{*}}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(S^{Y}_{n}\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}(S^{X}_{n}\in E_{t})\Big|,

\displaystyle I_{2}:=\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(S^{Y}_{n}\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}(S^{X}_{n}\in E_{t})\Big|.

By the triangle inequality,

\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(S^{Y}_{n}\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}(S^{X}_{n}\in E_{t})\Big|\leq I_{1}+I_{2}.

We show below that (for the chosen value of
D_{1}
),
t_{*}
is sufficiently large so that

I_{1}\leq\frac{1}{n}.

(120)

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}
from Lemma
1
. To bound
I_{2}
, for any
0<\varepsilon<s
let

\displaystyle I^{\varepsilon}_{2,1}:=\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\sup_{w\geq t-\varepsilon}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}S_{n}^{Y}\leq w)-{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}S_{n}^{X}\leq w)\Big|,

\displaystyle I^{\varepsilon}_{2,2}:=\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\sup_{w\geq t}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}S_{n}^{Y}\leq w)-{\mathrm{P}}(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}v^{\top}S_{n}^{Y}\leq w-\varepsilon)\Big|.

By Lemma
12
,
I_{2}\leq I^{\varepsilon}_{2,1}+I^{\varepsilon}_{2,2}
for any
\varepsilon<s
. Let
\varepsilon_{*}=1/n
, which requires the constraint
1/n<s
, assumed in the formulation of the theorem we are proving. We show below that there is a universal constant
C_{11}\geq 1
(this condition is a convenience for keeping track of constants and does not play an essential role in the proof) such that

I^{\varepsilon_{*}}_{2,1}\leq C_{11}\Big[H_{n}^{3}\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(s-C_{11}\log(n)\delta_{*},C_{11}\delta_{*})\vee\big\{\delta_{*}\sqrt{\log(n)}\big\}\Big]

(121)

for
t_{*}=D_{3}H_{n}\log(K+1)\log(n)
as defined above, and
\delta_{*}
as defined as in the formulation of the theorem we are proving. Now note that

I^{\varepsilon_{*}}_{2,2}\leq\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(s,1/n)\leq C_{11}\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(s-C_{11}\log(n)\delta_{*},C_{11}\delta_{*}).

(122)

The first inequality holds by definition, and the second holds because
\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}
is monotone non-increasing in its first argument, and monotone non-decreasing in its second argument. It follows that

\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(S^{Y}_{n}\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}(S^{X}_{n}\in E_{t})\Big|\leq 2C_{11}\Big[H_{n}^{3}\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(s-C_{11}\log(n)\delta_{*},C_{11}\delta_{*})\vee\big\{\delta_{*}\sqrt{\log(n)}\big\}\Big]+\frac{1}{n}

Set the universal constant
D
in the formulation of the theorem as
D=3C_{11}
.Once Equations (
120
) and (
121
) are justified, the proof will be complete.

Justification of Equation (
121
)

To bound
I^{\varepsilon_{*}}_{2,1}
, we will apply Theorem
3
to the collection of random variables
\{v^{\top}X\}_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon_{*},t}}
. Let
C_{13}=\Big[(C_{4,1}\vee C_{3,1})\sqrt{C_{\psi_{1/2}}}\Big]\vee 1
, where
C_{4,1}
and
C_{3,1}
are the universal constants from Lemma
42
and
C_{\psi_{1/2}}
is a universal constant from Lemma
40
. As a first step we show that
(C_{13}H_{n})^{3}
satisfies the constraints (
106
) –(
108
). Observe that

\max_{i=1}^{n}\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\|\pi_{k}X_{i}\|_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\sqrt{C_{\psi_{1/2}}}\max_{i=1}^{n}\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}\|[\pi_{k}X_{i}]_{j}\|^{2}_{\Psi_{1}}}\leq\sqrt{C_{\psi_{1/2}}}H_{n},

(123)

where we have used Lemmas
42
and
40
. By Lemma
1
, if
v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}
, there exists a unique integer
k
(satisfying
1\leq k\leq K
) such that
\pi_{k}\neq 0
. Given a vector
v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}
, let
l(v)
be this unique integer, so that
l(v)\in\{1,\dots,K\}
. Note that for all
v
,
\pi_{l(v)}v\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
is a unit vector.
Equation (
123
) and Lemmas
42
(ii) and
40
show that for any
\varepsilon<t
(we must have
\varepsilon<t
so that
\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}
is defined),

\begin{split}\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|v^{T}X_{i}|^{4}]=&\max_{k=1}^{K}\max_{(v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t})\wedge(l(v)=k)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|(\pi_{k}v)^{\top}(\pi_{k}X_{i})|^{4}]\\
\leq&\max_{k=1}^{K}\max_{(v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t})\wedge(l(v)=k)}\max_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|(\pi_{k}v)^{\top}(\pi_{k}X_{i})|^{4}]\\
\leq&\max_{k=1}^{K}\max_{(v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t})\wedge(l(v)=k)}\max_{i=1}^{n}\Big(C_{4,1}\|(\pi_{k}v)^{\top}(\pi_{k}X_{i})\|_{\Psi_{1}}\Big)^{4}\\
\leq&\max_{k=1}^{K}\max_{i=1}^{n}\Big(C_{4,1}\|\|\pi_{k}X_{i}\|_{2}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\Big)^{4}\\
\leq&\Big(C_{4,1}\sqrt{C_{\psi_{1/2}}}H_{n}\Big)^{4}\leq(C_{13}H_{n})^{6}.\end{split}

(124)

In the last line we used (
123
) and in the third line we used Lemma
42
(ii). We further use the property that if
0\leq X\leq Y
almost surely then also
\|X\|_{\psi}\leq\|Y\|_{\psi}
, which follows directly from the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\psi}
, in the fourth line. Likewise we have,

\begin{split}&\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon,t}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|v^{T}X_{i}|^{3}]\leq\Big(C_{3,1}\sqrt{C_{\psi_{1/2}}}H_{n}\Big)^{3}\leq(C_{13}H_{n})^{3}\\
&\max_{i=1}^{n}\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\|\pi_{k}X_{i}\|_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\sqrt{C_{\psi_{1/2}}}H_{n}\leq(C_{13}H_{n})^{3}.\end{split}

(125)

By Theorem
3
there is a universal constant
C_{2}
such that

\displaystyle I^{\varepsilon_{*}}_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\sup_{w\geq t-\varepsilon_{*}}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon_{*},t}}v^{\top}S_{n}^{Y}\leq w\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon_{*},t}}v^{\top}S_{n}^{X}\leq w\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}C_{2}\Big[\Big\{(C_{13}H_{n})^{3}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}\Big(s-\frac{1}{n}-C_{2}\log_{2}(n)\phi_{*},C_{2}\phi_{*}\Big)\Big\}\vee\big\{\phi_{*}\sqrt{\log(n)}\big\}\Big]

for

\phi_{*}=\Big(\frac{\log^{4}|V_{\varepsilon_{*},t}|(C_{13}H_{n})^{6}}{n}\Big)^{1/6}.

A straightforward calculation shows that there is a universal constant
C_{14}
such that for all
t\leq t_{*}
,

\log(|\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon_{*},t}|)\leq\log(|\mathcal{V}_{\varepsilon_{*},t_{*}}|)\leq C_{14}M\log(KnH_{n}).

Let
C_{15}=\big[C_{13}C_{14}\big]^{4/6}
. Then

C_{15}\delta_{*}=C_{15}\Big(\dfrac{H_{n}^{6}M^{4}\log^{4}(H_{n}Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}\geq\phi_{*}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\Big[\Big\{(C_{13}H_{n})^{3}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(s-n^{-1}-C_{2}\log_{2}(n)\phi_{*},C_{2}\phi_{*})\Big\}\vee\big\{\phi_{*}\sqrt{\log(n)}\big\}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\Big[\Big\{H_{n}^{3}\sup_{t\in[s,t_{*}]}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(s-C_{3}\log(n)\delta_{*},C_{3}\delta_{*})\Big\}\vee\big\{\delta_{*}\sqrt{\log(n)}\big\}\Big],

for sufficiently large universal constant
C_{3}
, where in the last line we used
1/n\leq\delta_{*}
,
n\geq 3
and
\log_{2}(n)\leq 2\log(n)
. This shows the bound in (
121
).

Justification of Equation (
120
)

By the triangle inequality,

I_{1}\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}\|_{2}>t_{*}\Big)+{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{Y}_{n}\|_{2}>t_{*}\Big).

(126)

By Lemma
41
(iv),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}\|_{2}>t_{*}\Big)+{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{Y}_{n}\|_{2}>t_{*}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{t_{*}}{\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}\|_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}}\Big)+2\exp\Big(-\frac{t_{*}}{\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{Y}_{n}\|_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}}\Big).

Therefore it suffices to show that

t_{*}\geq\Big(\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{Y}_{n}\|_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}\log(4n)\Big)\vee\Big(\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}\|_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}\log(4n)\Big).

(127)

First, note that

\displaystyle\Big\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}\|_{2}\Big\|_{\Psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq K_{\psi_{1}}\log(K+1)\max_{k=1}^{K}\Bigg\|\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}([\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}]_{j})^{2}}\Bigg\|_{\Psi_{1}}

(Lemma
41
(ii))

\displaystyle=K_{\psi_{1}}\log(K+1)\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{M}([\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}]_{j})^{2}\Big\|_{\Psi_{1/2}}}

(Lemma
42
(i))

\displaystyle\leq K_{\psi_{1}}\log(K+1)\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}C_{\psi_{1/2}}\Big\|([\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}]_{j})^{2}\Big\|_{\Psi_{1/2}}}

(Lemma
40
)

\displaystyle=K_{\psi_{1}}\sqrt{C_{\psi_{1/2}}}\log(K+1)\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}\Big\|[\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}]_{j}\Big\|^{2}_{\Psi_{1}}}

(Lemma
42
(i))

\displaystyle=K_{\psi_{1}}\sqrt{C_{\psi_{1/2}}}\log(K+1)\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}\big(K_{1}\max_{i=1}^{n}\Big\|[\pi_{k}X_{i}]_{j}\Big\|_{\Psi_{1}}\big)^{2}}

(Lemma
41
(iii))

\displaystyle\leq K^{2}_{1}K_{\psi_{1}}\sqrt{C_{\psi_{1/2}}}\log(K+1)H_{n}.

In the final line, we used the definition of
H_{n}
and the bound

\sum_{j=1}^{M}\Big\{\big\|[\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}]_{j}\big\|_{\Psi_{1}}\Big\}^{2}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{M}\Big\{K_{1}\max_{i=1,\dots,n}\big\|[\pi_{k}X_{i}]_{j}\big\|_{\Psi_{1}}\Big\}^{2}=K_{1}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\max_{i=1,\dots,n}\big\|[\pi_{k}X_{i}]_{j}\big\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}

which follows by Lemma
41
(iii) since the
X_{i}
are centered by assumption.

Then note that
\|[\pi_{k}Y_{i}]_{j}\|^{2}_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C_{4}\|[\pi_{k}X_{i}]_{j}\|^{2}_{\Psi_{1}}
for a universal constant
C_{4}
since

\displaystyle\|[\pi_{k}Y_{i}]_{j}\|_{\Psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|1\|_{\psi_{2}}\|[\pi_{k}Y_{i}]_{j}\|_{\Psi_{2}}=\|1\|_{\psi_{2}}\sqrt{\Var([\pi_{k}Y_{i}]_{j})}\sqrt{8/3}=\|1\|_{\psi_{2}}\sqrt{[\Var([\pi_{k}X_{i}]_{j})}\sqrt{8/3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|[\pi_{k}X_{i}]_{j}\big\|_{\Psi_{1}}.

The first inequality follows from Lemma
42
(i) and the first inequality because each
Y_{i}
is Gaussian. This bound implies that

\Big\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}\|_{2}\Big\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C_{5}\log(K+1)H_{n}

by the same reasoning used to bound
\Big\|\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}S^{X}_{n}\|_{2}\Big\|_{\Psi_{1}}
and a universal constant
C_{5}
. Then set
D_{1}
in the definition of
t_{*}
so that (
127
) is satisfied.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
13

Recall the notation introduced in Section
S.4
.

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(T^{M,G}\leq t)-{\mathrm{P}}(T^{M}\leq t)\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}g\|_{2}^{2}\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\Big\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i,k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\leq t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}g\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{t}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\Big\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i,k}\Big\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{t}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\geq\sqrt{s}}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}g\|_{2}\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\Big\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i,k}\Big\|_{2}\leq t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\geq\sqrt{s}}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(g\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}\in E_{t}\Big)\Big|

where
\Phi_{i,k}
is defined through
\pi_{k}\Phi=:\Phi_{i,k}
, as previously noted in the second paragraph of section
S.4
.

Let
D_{2}=D\vee 2
for the constant
D
of Theorem
4
, and let
D_{1}
be the constant by the same name of Theorem
4
. Under (A2) we have by
Lemma
34

\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}\max_{i=1}^{n}\|[\pi_{k}\Phi_{i}]_{j}\|^{2}_{\Psi_{1}}}\leq C_{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}}.

(128)

Therefore the factor
C_{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}}
satisfies (
119
), and we may set
H_{n}
in the statement of Theorem
4
as
H_{n}=\big(C_{\phi}\vee 1\big)\sqrt{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}}
. Note that
H_{n}>1
. Let
\delta_{*}=\big(\dfrac{H_{n}^{6}M^{4}\log^{4}(H_{n}Kn)}{n}\big)^{1/6}
. There are now two cases. If
\sqrt{s}\leq D_{2}\log_{2}(n)\delta_{*}/2
then,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\sqrt{s}}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(g\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}\in E_{t}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{D_{2}\log_{2}(n)\delta_{*}}{\sqrt{s}}

\displaystyle\leq H_{n}\frac{4D_{2}}{\sqrt{s}}\log(n)\Big(\dfrac{M^{4}\log^{4}(H_{n}Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}

\displaystyle\leq 4D_{2}H_{n}[1+\log(H_{n})]^{4/6}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}\Big(\dfrac{M^{4}[\log(n)]^{6}\log^{4}(Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}

\displaystyle\leq 4D_{2}H_{n}[1+\log(H_{n})]^{4/6}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s\wedge 1}}\Big(\dfrac{M^{10}\log^{10}(Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6},

and so Equation (
117
) holds with
C=C^{(1)}=4D_{2}H_{n}[1+\log(H_{n})]^{4/6}
. If
\sqrt{s}>D_{2}\log_{2}(n)\delta_{*}/2
, a straightforward computation shows that
\sqrt{s}>1/n
for all
n\geq 3
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{s}>D_{2}\log_{2}(n)\delta_{*}/2>\log_{2}(n)n^{-1/6}=n^{-1}\log_{2}(n)n^{5/6}>n^{-1}

where in the last line we used that
\log_{2}(n)n^{5/6}\geq 1
if
n\geq 3
.

By Theorem
4
, for
t_{*}=D_{1}H_{n}\log(K+1)\log(n)
, where
H_{n}
is any number that satisfies (
119
) and
H_{n}\geq 1
,

\sup_{t\geq\sqrt{s}}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(g\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}\in E_{t}\Big)\Big|\\
\leq D_{2}\Big[\Big\{\sup_{t\in[\sqrt{s},t_{*}]}H_{n}^{3}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(\sqrt{s}-D_{2}\log(n)\delta_{*},D_{2}\delta_{*})\Big\}\vee\big\{\delta_{*}\sqrt{\log(n)}\big\}\Big],

where

\mathcal{A}_{n}^{t}(w,\delta):=\sup_{x\geq w}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\frac{1}{n},t}}v^{\top}g-x\Big|\leq\delta\Big).

We used that
\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}
is non-increasing in its first argument, and non-decreasing in its second argument when replacing
D
by
D_{2}
in the above bound.
Apply Lemma
7
to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[\sqrt{s},t_{*}]}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{t}(\sqrt{s}-D_{2}\log(n)\delta_{*},D_{2}\delta_{*})\leq\sup_{t\in[\sqrt{s},t_{*}]}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{t}(\sqrt{s}/2,D_{2}\delta_{*})\leq 34\frac{D_{2}\delta_{*}}{\sqrt{s}}\sup_{t\in[\sqrt{s},t_{*}]}\log(|\mathcal{V}_{1/n,t}|).

By Lemma
1
(a),

\sup_{t\in[\sqrt{s},t_{*}]}\log(|\mathcal{V}_{1/n,t}|)\leq 3M\log(K(1+t_{*}n))\leq C_{\phi,\alpha}M\log(Kn),

where in the last inequality we plugged in the value of
t_{*}
and noted that for a constant
C_{\phi,\alpha}
that is universal for fixed values of
\phi
and
\alpha
we have
3M\log(K(1+t_{*}n))\leq C_{\phi,\alpha}M\log(Kn)
. The result now follows from plugging in the definition of
\delta_{*}
, and noting that the constant
H_{n}
inside of the logarithmic term can be pulled out of the argument of the logarithm and absorbed into
C_{\phi,\alpha}
.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
14

We follow the proof of Lemma
13
, which shows that it suffices to bound

\sup_{t\geq\sqrt{s}}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(g\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}\in E_{t}\Big)\Big|,

and defines the sequence
H_{n}
and the constant
D_{2}
and
\delta_{*}
. Repeating the argument made in the proof of Lemma

, if
\sqrt{s}\leq D_{2}\log_{2}(n)\delta_{*}/2
then,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\sqrt{s}}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(g\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}\in E_{t}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{D_{2}\log_{2}(n)\delta_{*}}{\sqrt{s}}

\displaystyle\leq H_{n}\frac{4D_{2}}{\sqrt{s}}\log(n)\Big(\dfrac{M^{4}\log^{4}(H_{n}Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}

\displaystyle\leq 4D_{2}H_{n}[1+\log(H_{n})]^{4/6}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}\Big(\dfrac{M^{4}[\log(n)]^{6}\log^{4}(Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}.

and so Equation (
118
) holds with
C=C^{(1)}=4D_{2}H_{n}[1+\log(H_{n})]^{4/6}
. If
\sqrt{s}>D_{2}\log_{2}(n)\delta_{*}/2
, a straightforward computation shows that
\sqrt{s}>1/n
for all
n\geq 3
.

By Theorem
4
, for
t_{*}=D_{1}H_{n}\log(K+1)\log(n)
, where
H_{n}
is any number that satisfies (
119
) and
H_{n}\geq 1
,

\sup_{t\geq\sqrt{s}}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}(g\in E_{t})-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}\in E_{t}\Big)\Big|\\
\leq D_{2}\Big[\Big\{\sup_{t\in[\sqrt{s},t_{*}]}H_{n}^{3}\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}(\sqrt{s}-D_{2}\log(n)\delta_{*},D_{2}\delta_{*})\Big\}\vee\big\{\delta_{*}\sqrt{\log(n)}\big\}\Big],

where

\mathcal{A}_{n}^{t}(w,\delta):=\sup_{x\geq w}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{\frac{1}{n},t}}v^{\top}g-x\Big|\leq\delta\Big).

We used that
\mathcal{A}^{t}_{n}
is non-increasing in its first argument, and non-decreasing in its second argument when replacing
D
by
D_{2}
in the above bound. Apply Lemma
11
(with
\varepsilon=1/n
, noting that
\delta_{*}
is (up to a numeric constant) larger than
1/n
) to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[\sqrt{s},t_{*}]}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{t}(\sqrt{s}-D_{2}\log(n)\delta_{*},D_{2}\delta_{*})\leq\sup_{t\in[\sqrt{s},t_{*}]}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{t}(\sqrt{s}/2,D_{2}\delta_{*})

\displaystyle\leq F_{\alpha,\beta,\sqrt{s},C_{\phi},C_{2}}\Big[D_{2}\Big(\frac{M^{4}\log^{4}(Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}\vee 1/n\Big]\log^{1+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(n)

\displaystyle\leq D_{2}F_{\alpha,\beta,\sqrt{s},C_{\phi},C_{2}}\Big(\frac{M^{4}\log^{4}(Kn)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}\log^{1+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(n),

and so Equation (
118
) holds for sufficiently large constant
C
. We have set the parameter
m
equal to
n
in our application of Lemma
11
.
\Box

S.6.7
Gaussian Comparison Bounds for
L^{\infty}-L^{2}
Mixed Norms

We begin the section by proving two Lemmas that are used in the proof of Theorem
1
. Recall the definition

\Delta_{Me,MG}(s;f):=\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}_{e}(T^{M,e}(\Phi,e)\leq t)-{\mathrm{P}}(T^{M,G}(g)\leq t)\Big|.

from the proof of Theorem
1
. We also introduce some additional notation used below. Let
\Phi_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{KM}
have entries
\phi_{j}^{f_{k,i}},j=1,\dots,M
in the
k
’th copy of
\mathbb{R}^{M}
. Let
\Sigma_{i}^{\Phi}
denote the covariance matrix of
\Phi_{i}
and let
\Sigma^{\Phi}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma^{\Phi}_{i}
. Let
\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi}
denote the empirical covariance matrix of
\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}
. The main result of this section is the following. It’s proof relies on intermediate technical results that are stated and proved further below.

Lemma 15
.

Suppose conditions (A1)–(A3) hold. There exists a constant
C_{gc}
that may depend on
\alpha
and
C_{\phi}
but is universal for fixed values of those parameters, such that for all
n
satisfying
n\geq 3
and all
K\geq 1
and all
M\geq 3
we have

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Delta_{Me,MG}(s;f)>\frac{C_{gc}}{\sqrt{s}\wedge 1}\Big[M^{4/3}\log(Kn)\Big(\frac{\log(KMn)\log^{2}(n)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}\Big]\Big)\leq 1/n.

(129)

Lemma 16
.

Suppose conditions (A1)–(A4) hold. There exists a constant
C_{1}
that may depend on
C_{\phi}
,
C_{2}
,
\alpha
,
\beta
, and
s
such that for all
K\geq 1
, and all
n
satisfying
n\geq 3
and and all
M\geq 3
, we have

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Delta_{Me,MG}(s;f)>C_{1}\Big[M^{2/3}\log^{1/3}(Kn)\Big(\frac{\log(KMn)\log^{2}(n)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}\Big]\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(n)\Big)\\
\leq 1/n.

(130)

The following two results appear in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
.

Lemma 17
.

Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be independent with covariance matrices
\Sigma_{1},\dots,\Sigma_{n}
, and let
\Sigma=n^{-1}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma_{i})
. Let
\widehat{\Sigma}
denote the sample covariance matrix of
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
. Assume that
B_{n}
satisfies (
106
)–(
108
). There exists a universal constant
C
(not depending on
\tau
) such that for all
\tau\in(0,e^{-1})
,

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\|\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}\|_{\infty}>C[n^{-1}B_{n}^{2}\log(pn)\log^{2}(1/\tau)]^{1/2}\Big)\leq\tau.

The proof is given within the proof of Proposition 4.1, case (E.1), in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
. Let

\widetilde{F}_{\beta}(w,x):=\beta^{-1}\log\left({\textstyle\sum}_{j=1}^{p}\exp\left(\beta(w_{j}-x_{j})\right)\right),\ w\in\mathbb{R}^{p},x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}.

(131)

The next result is a slight modification of Theorem 1 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2015
which is also mentioned in the proof of Proposition 4.1 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
.

Lemma 18
(Modification of
Chernozhukov et al. 2015
, Theorem 1).

Let
g\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
with uniformly bounded first and second derivatives, and let
F_{\beta}
be the smooth-max function in (
131
). Suppose that
p>2
. Fix
\beta>0
. Let
X,Y\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
with
X\sim N(0,\Sigma_{1})
, and
Y\sim N(0,\Sigma_{2})
. Then

\sup_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}|\mathbb{E}[g(\widetilde{F}_{\beta}(X,w))-\mathbb{E}[g(\widetilde{F}_{\beta}(Y,w))]|\leq(\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}/2+\beta\|g^{\prime}\|_{\infty})\|\Sigma_{1}-\Sigma_{2}\|_{\infty}.

The following Lemma follows from the previous one and an argument similar to the the one made leading up to (
S.6.5
), and the proof is included below.

Lemma 19
.

Let
X,Y\in\mathbb{R}^{p}(p\geq 3)
with
X\sim N(0,\Sigma_{1})
, and
Y\sim N(0,\Sigma_{2})
. Let
\Delta=\|\Sigma_{1}-\Sigma_{2}\|_{\infty}
. Then for any
\phi>0
and any
x\in\mathbb{R}
, there is a universal constant
C
which does depend on
\Sigma_{1}
,
\Sigma_{2}
,
\phi
, or
p
or
x
such that

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}X_{j}\leq x\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}\leq x\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j=1}^{p}Y_{j}-x\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)\vee{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j=1}^{p}Y_{j}-x-2\phi^{-1}\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)+C\phi^{2}\log(p)\Delta.

Lemma 20
.

Let
X,Y\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
with
X\sim N(0,\Sigma_{1})
, and
Y\sim N(0,\Sigma_{2})
. Let
\Delta=\|\Sigma_{1}-\Sigma_{2}\|_{\infty}
. Suppose there is a factor
D=D_{m}(\Sigma_{1},t,m)
such that for any
m\in\mathbb{N},m\geq 3
and all
\tau\geq 0
,

\sup_{x\geq t/2}{\mathrm{P}}(|\max_{j=1}^{p}X_{j}-x|\leq\tau)\leq D\big(\tau\vee 1/m\big).

(132)

There is a numeric (universal) constant
C
(independent of
D
and all other parameters) such that for any
m\geq 3

\sup_{x\geq t}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}X_{j}\leq x\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}\leq x\Big)\Big|\leq C\Big(D^{2/3}\vee\frac{1}{t\wedge 1}\Big)\big(\{\Delta\log(p)\}\vee m^{-2}\big)^{1/3}.

(133)

Proposition 1
.

Let
X
and
Y
be centered Gaussians on
(\mathbb{R}^{M})^{K}

(M\geq 3,K\geq 1)
. Let
\Sigma_{1}=\Var(X)
, and
\Sigma_{2}=\Var(Y)
. Suppose
s>0
and suppose
0<\varepsilon<1/\exp(1)
. Further, let

U=\big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\Tr\{\Var(\pi_{k}Y)\}}\big)\vee\big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\Tr\{\Var(\pi_{k}X)\}}\big)

There is a universal constant
C
(independent of all parameters) such that

\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}Y\|_{2}\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}X\|_{2}\leq t\Big)\Big|\\
\leq C\frac{1+\log(U\vee 1)}{(s\wedge 1)}M\Big[M^{1/3}\|{\Sigma}_{1}-\Sigma_{2}\|_{\infty}^{1/3}+\varepsilon\Big]\log\Big(\frac{K}{\varepsilon}\Big).

Proposition 2
.

Let
X
and
Y
be centered Gaussians on
(\mathbb{R}^{M})^{K}

(M\geq 3,K\geq 1)
, and let
m\in\mathbb{N},m\geq 3
. Let
\Sigma_{1}=\Var(X)
, and
\Sigma_{2}=\Var(Y)
, and suppose that
\Sigma_{2}
has the properties indicated in Lemma
10
. Suppose
s>0
and suppose
0<\varepsilon<1/\exp(1)
. Let

U=\big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\Tr\{\Var(\pi_{k}Y)\}}\big)\vee\big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\Tr\{\Var(\pi_{k}X)\}}\big)

Then there is a constant
D_{\alpha,\beta,s,C_{1},C_{2}}
that is universal for fixed values of the parameters and independent of
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
in its subscript such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq s}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}X\|_{2}\leq t\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}Y\|_{2}\leq t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq D_{\alpha,\beta,s,C_{1},C_{2}}(1+\log(U\vee 1))^{\frac{1}{3}}\Big[M^{2/3}\|\Sigma_{1}-\Sigma_{2}\|_{\infty}^{1/3}\log^{1/3}\Big(\frac{K}{\varepsilon}\Big)+(\varepsilon\vee m^{-2/3})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(m).

S.6.8
Proof for section
S.6.7

Proof of Lemma
20

Note that if
D
satisfies (
132
), then
D_{2}=D\wedge{m}
also satisfies (
132
). Let
D_{3}=1\vee D_{2}
. Let
\tau_{*}=(\log(p)\Delta/D_{3})^{1/3}
. If
\tau_{*}\geq t/4
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{x\geq t}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}X_{j}\leq x\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}\leq x\Big)\Big|\leq\frac{4\tau_{*}}{t}\leq 4\frac{(\log(p)\Delta)^{1/3}}{tD_{3}^{1/3}}\leq 4\frac{\big(\log(p)\Delta\big)^{1/3}}{t}

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{\big(\log(p)\Delta\big)^{1/3}}{1\wedge t}\leq 4\Big(D^{2/3}\vee\frac{1}{t\wedge 1}\Big)\big(\{\Delta\log(p)\}\vee m^{-2}\big)^{1/3}.

Let
C^{(1)}=4
. From now on we assume
\tau_{*}<t/4
. By Lemma
19
applied with
\phi=\tau_{*}^{-1}
we have for a numeric constant
C_{1}
,

\sup_{x\geq t}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}X_{j}\leq x\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}\leq x\Big)\Big|\leq\sup_{x\geq t/2}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{Y}]_{j}-x\Big|\leq\tau_{*}\Big)+C_{1}\tau_{*}^{-2}\Delta\log(p).

(134)

First consider the case
\tau_{*}<1/{m}
. Then by (
134
)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\geq t}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}X_{j}\leq x\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}\leq x\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\geq t/2}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{Y}]_{j}-x\Big|\leq 1/{m}\Big)+C_{1}\tau_{*}^{-2}\log(p)\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D_{3}}{{m}}+C_{1}[\Delta\log(p)]^{1/3}[D_{3}]^{2/3}

\displaystyle=D_{3}^{2/3}\Big[C_{1}\big(\log(p)\Delta\big)^{1/3}+D_{3}^{1/3}/{m}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq D_{3}^{2/3}\Big[C_{1}\big(\log(p)\Delta\big)^{1/3}+{m}^{-2/3}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2(C_{1}\vee 1)[D_{3}]^{2/3}\Big[\big(\log(p)\Delta\big)^{1/3}+{m}^{-2/3}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2(C_{1}\vee 1)[D\vee 1]^{2/3}\Big[\big(\log(p)\Delta\big)^{1/3}+{m}^{-2/3}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2(C_{1}\vee 1)\Big(D^{2/3}\vee\frac{1}{t\wedge 1}\Big)\Big[\big(\log(p)\Delta\big)^{1/3}+{m}^{-2/3}\Big]

where we used the definition of
\tau_{*}
and the assumption in (
132
) in the second inequality. Let
C^{(2)}=4(C_{1}\vee 1)
. Now consider the case
\tau_{*}\geq 1/{m}
. By (
134
)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\geq t}\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}X_{j}\leq x\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}\leq x\Big)\Big|\leq\sup_{x\geq t/2}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{j=1}^{p}[S_{n}^{Y}]_{j}-x\Big|\leq\tau^{*}\Big)+C_{1}\tau_{*}^{-2}\Delta\log(p)

\displaystyle\leq[D_{3}]\tau_{*}+C_{1}[\Delta\log(p)]^{1/3}[D_{3}]^{2/3}\leq(1+C_{1})[D_{3}]^{2/3}[\Delta\log(p)]^{1/3}

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{1})\Big(D^{2/3}\vee\frac{1}{t\wedge 1}\Big)\Big(\{\Delta\log(p)\}\vee{m}^{-2}\Big)^{1/3},

where we used the definition of
\tau_{*}
and the assumption in (
132
) in the second inequality and the definition of
\tau_{*}
again in the third inequality.
Set
C^{(3)}=(1+C_{1})
, and set
C
in the formulation of the Lemma as
C=C^{(1)}\vee C^{(2)}\vee C^{(3)}
. This completes the proof of Lemma
20
.

\Box

Proof of Lemma
15
and Lemma
16

By Lemma
17
, for a universal constant
C
and any
\tau\in(0,e^{-1})

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\|\Sigma^{\Phi}-\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi}\|_{\infty}>C[n^{-1}\log(KMn)\log^{2}(1/\tau)]^{1/2}\Big)\leq\tau,

(135)

where we plugged in
KM
in place of
p
in the bound of Lemma
17
.
We also used the fact that under (A2)
B_{n}
in Lemma
17
can bounded from above by a constant that is independent of
n
and depends only on the constants
C_{\phi},\alpha
appearing in (A2). Next, let
(\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi})_{k}
denote the
k^{\prime}th
diagonal block of size
M\times M
. We may assume without loss of generality assume that

\sqrt{M}C[n^{-1}\log(KMn)\log^{2}(n)]^{1/2}\leq 1

since otherwise the bound in (
129
) can be made to hold by picking
C_{gc}
sufficiently large. Then on the event
\Omega_{1}:=\{\|\Sigma^{\Phi}-\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi}\|_{\infty}\leq C[n^{-1}\log(KMn)\log^{2}(n)]^{1/2}\}
, which holds with probability greater than
1-1/n
by (
135
),

\displaystyle\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}[(\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi})_{k}]_{jj}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{M}\|\Sigma^{\Phi}-\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi}\|_{\infty}+\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}[(\Sigma)_{k}]_{jj}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{M}C[n^{-1}\log(KMn)\log^{2}(n)]^{1/2}+U\leq 2U

where

U:=1\vee\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M}[(\Sigma^{\Phi})_{k}]_{jj}}

is bounded by a constant that depends on
C_{\phi},\alpha
only by (A2).

Now let
X
denote a centered Gaussian vector in
\mathbb{R}^{KM}
with covariance matrix
\Sigma^{\Phi}
and let
Y=[\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi}]^{1/2}Z
for a isotropic Gaussian
Z
on
\mathbb{R}^{KM}
. The distribution of
T^{M,e}(\Phi,e)
, conditional on
\mathcal{D}
is equal to the conditional distribution of
\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}Y\|_{2}^{2}
given
[\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi}]^{1/2}
while
T^{M,G}
has the same distribution as
\max_{k=1}^{K}\|\pi_{k}X\|_{2}^{2}
. Let

U_{n}:=1\vee\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\Tr\{(\Var(\pi_{k}\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi})\}}\vee\max_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\Tr\{(\Var(\pi_{k}\Sigma^{\Phi})\}}.

Given the earlier discussion,
U_{n}
is bounded by a constant that depends on
C_{\phi},\alpha
on the event
\Omega_{1}
.

To prove Lemma
15
, apply Proposition
1
with
\varepsilon=1/n
to obtain the existence of a
C
that may depend only on
\alpha
and
C_{\phi}
such that almost surely for any
n\geq 3
,

\Delta_{Me,MG}(f;s)\leq C\frac{1}{(\sqrt{s}\wedge 1)}\Big[M^{4/3}\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi}-\Sigma^{\Phi}\|_{\infty}^{1/3}+\frac{M}{n}\Big]\log(Kn),

where we absorbed the term
1+\log(U_{n})
into
C
based on the previously indicated bound on
B_{n}
. The claim follows by combining this bound with the bound
\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi}-\Sigma^{\Phi}\|_{\infty}
derived earlier and picking
C_{gc}
to be sufficiently large.

To prove Lemma
16
, apply Proposition
2
with
\varepsilon=1/n
, noting that under (A2) and (A4), each
\Phi_{i}
has the properties indicated in Lemma
10
. It follows that

\displaystyle\Delta_{Me,MG}(X;s)

\displaystyle\leq D_{\alpha,\beta,s,C_{1},C_{2}}(1+\log(U\vee 1))^{\frac{1}{3}}\Big[M^{2/3}\|\Sigma_{1}-\Sigma_{1}\|_{\infty}^{1/3}\log^{1/3}(Kn)+\frac{1}{n^{2/3}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\log^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(K+1)\log^{\frac{3\beta}{2\alpha-1}}(n)

on
\Omega_{1}
.
The claim follows by combining this bound with the bounds on
U_{n}
and
\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{\Phi}-\Sigma^{\Phi}\|_{\infty}
derived earlier, and picking
C_{1}
sufficiently large.

\Box

Proof of Lemma
19

Recall the notation in the proof leading up to Equation (
S.6.5
). Arguing as was done to obtain Equation (
S.6.5
) and applying Lemma
18
with
g(x)=g_{0}(\phi x),\beta=\phi\log p
for a fixed, three times continuously differentiable
g_{0}
and with
\beta=\phi\log p
gives the following bound:

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}X_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}\leq x-\phi^{-1}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}-x\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)\vee{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}-x-2\phi^{-1}\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{E}[g(F_{\beta}(X,x))-\mathbb{E}[g(F_{\beta}(Y,x))]\Big|

\displaystyle={\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}-x\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)\vee{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}-x-2\phi^{-1}\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{E}[g(\widetilde{F}_{\beta}(X,\boldsymbol{1}x))-\mathbb{E}[g(\widetilde{F}_{\beta}(Y,\boldsymbol{1}x))]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}-x\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)\vee{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}-x-2\phi^{-1}\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\Big(\frac{\|g_{0}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}}{2}\phi^{2}+\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\phi^{2}\log(p)\Big)\Delta

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}-x\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)\vee{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big|\max_{1\leq j\leq p}Y_{j}-x-2\phi^{-1}\Big|\leq\phi^{-1}\Big)+C\phi^{2}\Delta\log p

where the constant
C
in the last line depends on the derivatives of
g_{0}
and we defined
\boldsymbol{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
as a vector with all entries equal to
1
. Here, we applied arguments similar to those that were used to derive Equation (
S.6.5
